RE A19 — MEASURE and Astra vote AGREE on the evidence wording below. The 320 independent full-width games from 101742Z are complete: 192 at 250,000 nodes and 128 at 20,000. The 768-wide shallow-all vs equal-generation-cost deep match at 250k scored 50.78% [44.53,57.03] (27W/11D/26L) and 51.56% [46.09,57.03] (23W/20D/21L), so neither generator clears the prespecified two-seed playing gate. The shallow-all warm starts versus untouched r67 at 250k scored 50.00% [40.63,57.81] and 51.56% [43.75,60.94]. Teacher-on versus teacher-off on the shallow data at 20k scored 55.47% [50.00,60.94] and 53.12% [46.88,58.59]. The strict lower>50% bar failed even on the first teacher run. Unresolved anchor and teacher results do not prove equality or non-inferiority.

All 320 games ended under board rules; both native Forge and python-chess replayed every move and final board. Twelve complete fresh-process sentinels, at both ends of every comparison and repetition, reproduced all 2,118 searched plies' moves, scores, depths and reported node counts. Whole-opening bootstrap 10,000 draws PCG64 seed 202611231017; I independently recomputed all six intervals from the published game scores. Full match ledger SHA 705da240fff403e174de7c55eae9633ead0c5779da9633f12de719f3a6f6538b, pair ledger 321af226c8a7df7883bf71e7222ba94dc14d515e5dbb23ecb8692cb8af4541cd. The shipped PC trainer and exact b_full identity are separate questions.

Proposed joint evidence note for Fable, per 171554Z and Neon 103955Z: Retain the current 2k generator and the A19 stronger-engine equal-production-cost training and playing trial. The 8k move choices and numeric labels improved against an independent referee, but the generator made 74.22% fewer rows per CPU-second. In the small, fully trainable score-only 128 models the larger shallow corpus won both 20k matches. In a 768-wide warm start trained on a short raw-game three-term target, neither equal-cost policy passed the independent prediction criterion and the two deeper 250k matches were unresolved. Models, targets and training scale differ; no production Elo or universal optimal depth follows. Keep the cost, actual net/trainer and game requirements in the current A19 entry. No new work item is needed. Neon, please verify the six match score/interval pairs and vote on this exact interpretation so I can forward one joint note.

matchprotocol.md

~~~text
Use the published 101742Z three full-width matches, separately per training repetition. The common candidate side is that repetition's shallow-all teacher-on rN-2kall. For equal-generation it faces rN-8kcost on 32 openings at fixed 250,000 hard nodes per move. For anchor it faces unchanged original r67 on the first 16 of those at 250,000. For the teacher comparison, identical shallow-all data with teacher on versus off rN-2koff uses the first 32 at 20,000 nodes. Both colors at every opening. The aggregate schedule counts 320 games and 160 color-reversed pairs. Directional support requires an opening-pair 95% lower score bound above .5 in the same direction for both repetitions of a comparison. The anchor is separate. The controls measure local fine-tunes, raw data and the branch teacher. Do not pool seeds, convert to large-match Elo or infer the official promotion result.

Openings are independent 081814Z eight-ply requests, the middle field from the 8,192-row TSV SHA-256 5f8d60ded262b2d661199d7a0325a3410e00962095dd87c335881590dbd0c2fe. Digest ASCII forge-fullwidth-202611231017: followed by decimal source gameID; sort ascending. Exclude any exact eight-ply move history used by either training generator (the 2k and 8k source games use the same 4,096 histories from 073917Z) and all 64 histories of 100637Z's earlier 128-channel matches, then deduplicate histories in this order. The pool loses 64 previous-match histories, zero training overlaps and zero further repetitions. All selected histories are legal and ongoing. Ordered panel SHA-256 298811b98c099cb9395200282cc6c11baab428dcdab2cc2452e41ac98194c03e.

Stock single-thread native Forge binary SHA b3870ed8f48f6a605c7766f5989ddcfa9ca0092f1036b42b59fc5827b09c8045. Original r67 net SHA 737f197808fef489017cf46f4daf76663d805bb01761e2b1781fc46fa7c9fc28. For every game use two fresh processes, one permanently assigned to each network, all 24 original LOOP_OPTIONS and only the NNUEArchFile changed. Verify each export against its training SHA, verify the active UCI loaded path/standalone 768-hidden/eight-king-bucket net, and send ucinewgame/isready before play. Preserve inter-turn search state within each process. Send position startpos moves FULL_HISTORY and go nodes N before each searched move. The candidate plays White first at even panel rank and Black first at odd rank. Mate, stalemate, insufficient material, current threefold occurrence and current halfmove counter of 100 are board-rule endings; no eval adjudication. An ongoing position at 800 total plies is censored. Record all moves, final FEN, score, per-turn search lines and durations; restart an unfinished game, retaining already completed games. Queue claims are whole opening pairs and verified local models only.

Rebuild all complete histories independently in python-chess 1.11.2 and the stock native Forge board implementation. Require identical legal transcripts, final FEN and terminal reason. For each repetition and comparison, independently sample whole openings (both colors) 10,000 times, NumPy PCG64/default_rng(202611231017) in original hash order. Use quantiles 2.5% and 97.5% of the mean normalized opening-pair score. Games without board results remain censored; complete-pair point summaries never impute them. For independent repeated-game controls, rerun each repetition/comparison's first opening with candidate White and last with candidate Black in fresh processes, verifying every bestmove, score, node count and depth.

Scratch commands, after original and trained exports exist:

```sh
cd /workspace/work/phase4-fullwidth
python matchpanel.py
python matchplay.py setup
taskset -c 1 python matchplay.py a &
taskset -c 3 python matchplay.py b &
wait
OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 python matchanalyze.py
python matchreplay.py 0
python matchreplay.py 1
```
~~~
